# Integration verification report

Generated by `scripts/Export.lean`. Do not edit by hand.

- Lean toolchain: `leanprover/lean4:v4.35.0`
- Mathlib revision: `56a005855ea17e0b0958f73ec801ae140b0a1679`
- Source digest: `sha256:e85967a3fa01f3bf0ef5cbc601244ae16d9ebf0bb0c5403336a402cdcaa9bad8`
- Accepted axioms: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`

An antiderivative rule `int(λx. F) ⟶ λx. G` (`calculus1#int`) states that
wherever `F` is defined near `t`, `G` is defined near `t` and `v ↦ G[x := v]`
has derivative `F[x := t]` at `t` (`HasDerivAt`), for every assignment of the
metavariables satisfying the rule's conditions. A recursive rule
`int(λx. F) ⟶ λx. G[int(λx. H)(x)]` states that the residual integrands are
defined near `t` and that, for every choice of antiderivatives of them, the
result is an antiderivative of `F`. The rules are the twenty RUBI 4.16.1 rules
of `OpenSymbolicRules/Integration` with no `Int` in their result, whose
conditions are `FreeQ` or `NeQ` and whose heads, the outer `Int` removed, are
in the registry, 60 further rules without residual integrals whose conditions
are expressible (including `EqQ`, `relation1#eq`), and 334 rules with residual
integrals (305 migrated); the other 5,843 rules are not yet considered. The reference rewriter applies a
conditional rule only when its substituted conditions are closed and decided
true by exact rational arithmetic (`OSR.Cond.decide?`), and does not apply
recursive rules.

## Findings

### `integration.reciprocal`

- counterexample: near x = -1 the integrand 1 · x^(-1) is defined, but ln(-1) is not, since transc1#ln is defined on the positive reals (Lean theorem reciprocal_counterexample_real). RUBI reads Log as the complex logarithm; a real antiderivative is ln|x|, which is outside the fragment.
- complex: counterexample at the branch cut of the principal logarithm: near x = -1 the integrand is defined, but the result is log x, and log is not continuous at -1 (it tends to -π i from below while log(-1) = π i), so the result has no derivative there (Lean theorem reciprocal_counterexample_complex).

### `integration.log_monomial`

- real: n must take an integer value, since the fragment interprets arith1#power for integer exponents only; the statement covers every point near which c x^n > 0.
- complex: counterexample at the branch cut of the principal logarithm: at c = n = 1 the integrand log x is defined near x = -1, but the result x log x - x is not continuous at -1 (Lean theorem logMonomial_counterexample_complex).
- matching: the optional wildcards c. and n. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.log_linear_over_x`

- counterexample: at b = n = 0 and c = 1 the integrand is defined near x = 1, but (2 b n)^(-1) is not (Lean theorems logLinearOverX_counterexample_real and logLinearOverX_counterexample_complex). The v0.1 rule has only FreeQ conditions. In Mathematica an integrand with a literal b = 0 is simplified before matching, so this instance does not arise there; OSR v1 matching performs no such evaluation. The rule needs the conditions b ≠ 0 and n ≠ 0, which this migration does not add.
- matching: the optional wildcards a., b., c. and n. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.log_log_over_x`

- counterexample: at a = b = n = p = 0 and c = d = 1 the integrand is defined near x = 1, but n^(-1) is not (Lean theorems logLogOverX_counterexample_real and logLogOverX_counterexample_complex). The rule needs n ≠ 0, which this migration does not add.
- real: even with n ≠ 0, ln x in the result is undefined for x < 0, where the integrand can be defined (n even, d > 0); not proved.
- matching: the optional wildcards a., b., c., d., n. and p. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.constant_times_linear`

- counterexample: at c = 0 the integrand a (b + c x) is defined everywhere, but (2 c)^(-1) is not (Lean theorems constTimesLinear_counterexample_real and constTimesLinear_counterexample_complex). The rule needs c ≠ 0, which this migration does not add.
- matching: the optional wildcard c. of the v0.1 pattern is an ordinary metavariable; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default value.

### `integration.power`

- exponent: the fragment interprets arith1#power for integer exponents only, so the statement covers the integer values of m; for other values the integrand is undefined.
- conditions: RUBI's NeQ[m, -1] becomes a relation1#neq condition, read semantically (both sides defined, values different); Mathematica's NeQ tests that the difference is not possibly zero. The reference rewriter does not apply conditional rules.
- matching: the optional wildcard m. of the v0.1 pattern is an ordinary metavariable; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default value.

### `integration.linear_power`

- counterexample: at a = 1 and b = m = 0 the condition m ≠ -1 holds and the integrand (a + b x)^m is defined everywhere, but (b (m + 1))^(-1) is not (Lean theorems linearPower_counterexample_real and linearPower_counterexample_complex). The rule needs b ≠ 0, which this migration does not add.
- conditions: RUBI's NeQ[m, -1] becomes a relation1#neq condition, read semantically (both sides defined, values different); Mathematica's NeQ tests that the difference is not possibly zero. The reference rewriter does not apply conditional rules.
- matching: the optional wildcards a. and b. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.power_log_linear`

- counterexample: at c = 1 and a = b = d = m = n = 0 the condition m ≠ -1 holds and the integrand is defined everywhere, but (d (m + 1))^(-1) is not (Lean theorems powerLogLinear_counterexample_real and powerLogLinear_counterexample_complex). The rule needs d ≠ 0, which this migration does not add.
- conditions: RUBI's NeQ[m, -1] becomes a relation1#neq condition, read semantically (both sides defined, values different); Mathematica's NeQ tests that the difference is not possibly zero. The reference rewriter does not apply conditional rules.
- matching: the optional wildcards a., b., c., d., m. and n. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.sin_log`

- real: the condition b^2 d^2 n^2 + 1 ≠ 0 always holds; n must take an integer value, and the statement covers every point near which c x^n > 0.
- complex: counterexample at the branch cut of the principal logarithm: at a = 0, b = c = d = n = 1 the integrand sin(log x) is defined near x = -1, but the result (x sin(log x) - x cos(log x))/2 jumps across the cut, by sin(π i) = i sinh π ≠ 0 (Lean theorem sinLog_counterexample_complex).
- conditions: RUBI's NeQ[b^2 d^2 n^2 + 1, 0] becomes a relation1#neq condition, read semantically (both sides defined, values different); Mathematica's NeQ tests that the difference is not possibly zero. The reference rewriter does not apply conditional rules.
- matching: the optional wildcards a., b., c., d. and n. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.cos_log`

- real: the condition b^2 d^2 n^2 + 1 ≠ 0 always holds; n must take an integer value, and the statement covers every point near which c x^n > 0.
- complex: counterexample at the branch cut of the principal logarithm: at a = 0, b = c = d = n = 1 the integrand cos(log x) is defined near x = -1, but the result (x cos(log x) + x sin(log x))/2 jumps across the cut (Lean theorem cosLog_counterexample_complex).
- conditions: RUBI's NeQ[b^2 d^2 n^2 + 1, 0] becomes a relation1#neq condition, read semantically (both sides defined, values different); Mathematica's NeQ tests that the difference is not possibly zero. The reference rewriter does not apply conditional rules.
- matching: the optional wildcards a., b., c., d. and n. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.power_sin_log`

- counterexample: at c = 1 and the other metavariables 0 the condition b^2 d^2 n^2 + (m + 1)^2 ≠ 0 holds and the integrand is defined everywhere, but the denominator b^2 d^2 e n^2 + e (m + 1)^2 is 0 (Lean theorems powSinLog_counterexample_real and powSinLog_counterexample_complex). The rule needs e ≠ 0, which this migration does not add.
- conditions: RUBI's NeQ[b^2 d^2 n^2 + (m + 1)^2, 0] becomes a relation1#neq condition, read semantically (both sides defined, values different); Mathematica's NeQ tests that the difference is not possibly zero. The reference rewriter does not apply conditional rules.
- matching: the optional wildcards a., b., c., d., e., m. and n. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.log_power_log`

- counterexample: at c = d = 1 and n = p = 0 the integrand log(c log(d x^n)^p) = log 1 is defined everywhere, but the residual integrand 1/log(d x^n) = 1/log 1 is not (Lean theorems logPowLog_counterexample_real and logPowLog_counterexample_complex). Its coefficient -n p vanishes there, but the residual integral is still undefined. The rule needs log(d x^n) ≠ 0 wherever the integrand is defined, which p ≠ 0 ensures; this migration does not add it.
- residual integrals: the result contains Int[…], exported as int(λx. H)(x) (the calculus1#int FMP form); the statement holds for every choice of antiderivatives of the residual integrands. The reference rewriter does not apply this rule.
- matching: the optional wildcards c., d., n. and p. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

### `integration.log_linear_power`

- conditions: RUBI's GtQ[p, 0] becomes p > 0 (relation1#gt) and IntegerQ[2 p] becomes 2 p ∈ ℤ (set1#in, setname1#Z); the fragment interprets arith1#power for integer exponents only, so the statement covers the integer values of p.
- independence: p is not in the v0.1 FreeQ list, but RUBI's GtQ[p, 0] only holds for a numeric p, so the v1 rule declares p independent of x, as the statement requires.
- complex: counterexample at the branch cut of the principal logarithm: at a = 0, b = c = n = p = 1 the integrand log x is defined near x = -1, but the result x log x - Φ(x) jumps across the cut for every continuous Φ (Lean theorem logLinPow_counterexample_complex).
- residual integrals: the result contains Int[…], exported as int(λx. H)(x) (the calculus1#int FMP form); the statement holds for every choice of antiderivatives of the residual integrands. The reference rewriter does not apply this rule.
- matching: the optional wildcards a., b., c., n. and p. of the v0.1 pattern are ordinary metavariables; the v1 rule does not match the instances with RUBI's default values.

## Strategy

Rules apply at the root of an `int` term (their bodies are field expressions,
to which no calculus rule applies); the result of a rule is a lambda, so
normalization takes at most one step (`OSR.Calculus.rewrite_result`). The 755 seeds below are the
conformance cases.
